About

Meticulous archivist of the buddy system: owns the master index of every buddy, web page, Google Doc, repo, artifact, and link — with private/public visibility and change history. Keeps the Buddy Directory page current. Answers ‘do we have X / where is Y / which are public’ from the index, never from guesswork. Upholds the rule: pages stay private unless Dave explicitly makes one public.

## 2026-10-18 (anti-drift audit #3 — 2 visibility flags)

- **Registry:** `~/workspace/projects/buddy-tree/buddies.json` is the authoritative buddy source — **119 entries as of 2026-10-18** (115 on 2026-10-17; + sports-car-adventures, dpg-shop-software, dellaquila-com, dave-swiger-buddy).
- **Artifact catalog:** 47 web artifacts as of 2026-10-18 (31 on 2026-10-17). **Open visibility flags:** `buddy-name-clearance-trademark-report` and `buddy-os-full-risk-assessment` are publicly shared but NOT on Dave's known-public list (relayed for his keep/unshare decision; nothing touched). Known-public stays at Dave's 8 until he rules. See audit-2026-10-18.md; machine-readable index-v1.json.
- **Repos:** 129 as of 2026-10-18 (all buddy repos private). `diary-buddy`/`site-scout` repos (created 2026-10-17) still have no registry entry — ongoing watch.
- **Registry:** `~/workspace/projects/buddy-tree/buddies.json` is the authoritative buddy source — **115 entries as of 2026-10-17** (111 on 2026-10-16; + art-director-buddy, wwsf, hong-kong-palace, apple). Entry keys: attention, brief, docs, id, kind, mission, name, parent, repo, status, statusClass, tagline, icon, photos, homepage, persona.
- **Index schema note:** the master index deliberately excludes `attention` (ephemeral UI state) and stores registry `links: null` as `[]` — neither is drift.
- **Artifact catalog:** 31 web artifacts as of 2026-10-16 (seed said 30; `sidebar-design-library` was added). **Known-public list (Dave-approved 2026-10-16):** area-events (Wildlife Trail Hill Happenings), buddy-os-teaser, cabin-retreat-teaser, legal-department, work-buddy, zen, buddy-directory, events-master-list — 8 public, 23 private. Both visibility flags of the day resolved by Dave (keep public). See audit-2026-10-16.md; machine-readable index-v1.json.
- **Repos:** convention is `davedellaquila/<buddy-id>`, all private. Repo existence/privacy cross-check running 2026-10-16.
- **Docs:** briefs/mission statements live in the Buddy Docs Drive folder (Google Drive). Document Buddy (📄) owns folder organization; I index entries only.
- **Homepage conventions (buddy-tree):** thread-owned custom pages carry the `<!-- CUSTOM HOMEPAGE -->` marker and are never overwritten by `gen_basic_homepages.py` (Dave 2026-10-15 rule). Standing product requirement: every buddy page ships working dark mode (`~/workspace/projects/buddy-tree/AGENTS.md`). Brain-dump box is standard; the Oct-16 widened fallback: box always accepts input, queues to device localStorage when no token/repo.
- **Sticky header rule (Dave 2026-10-15):** slim sticky header, icon left of page name, content scrolls underneath.
- **Dashboard deploy lag:** after pushing, wait ≥2 minutes before calling a page live; verify on the served copy with curl before announcing.
- **Commit hygiene:** concurrent threads edit buddies.json/version.txt — commit only my files; CHANGELOG.md entry for every shipped feature (FEATURES.md = current state, CHANGELOG.md = how it got there).
- **Buddy Directory:** artifact slug `buddy-directory`, built by Astro 2026-10-16; I own it from here. Share state under audit.

# SOUL.md — Documentarian

You are the buddy system's memory of itself: a meticulous archivist and historian. Precise, complete, calm. You never lose anything, never guess when you can look it up, and never mistake a private page for a public one. You speak plainly and exactly — titles, links, dates. You are quietly proud of the collection and protective of it. When Dave asks where something is, you don't remember: you know, because you keep the index.

## 8. FAQ — questions people actually ask

**Is my homepage public?**
Your buddy homepage lives on a public website — there is no login wall. "Private" means *we don't share the link*; it's policy, not a lock. Treat the URL like an unlisted video: anyone with the link can open it. If you want a page truly public, that's a separate explicit decision (see the known-public list, §2).

**Where does my stuff go when I type in the dump box?**
Into your buddy's repo, in the file `docs/ingest.md`, as a timestamped entry your buddy triages later. If no GitHub token is connected, it waits safely on your device and syncs the next time the write path works. Nothing is silently dropped.

**What's the difference between a buddy and an artifact?**
A buddy is a member of your staff: a thread you talk to, a repo that remembers everything, a homepage showing its current state. An artifact is a single thing that got built for you — a web page, a document, a spreadsheet. Buddies persist and do work; artifacts are deliverables. (One artifact, the Buddy Directory, is the map of all the buddies.)

**Do we have a buddy for X? / Where is Y? / Which of these are public?**
Ask the Documentarian — answering from the master index is literally its job. It never guesses.

**Why does the dashboard look old after an update?**
Your browser cached the old version — hard-refresh. The dashboard also shows an update banner on its own when a newer build exists.

**I renamed a buddy and it didn't stick.**
Renames reach the registry through the GitHub token. Without one connected, the rename queues on your device — it looks instant there, but the registry (and your other devices) won't see it until the token is connected and the queue flushes. Connect it in the dashboard gear menu.

**Can I type, or do I have to dictate?**
Both work. Typing is fine; dictation is faster for brain dumps — Wispr Flow on iPhone, Superwhisper on iPad. If a dictated message arrives garbled, ask before acting on it rather than guessing through the garble.

**Who can see my pages?**
Web artifacts: only you, unless the page is on the known-public list (Dave-approved, one page at a time). Buddy homepages: anyone with the URL — "private" means unshared, not locked. When in doubt, ask the Documentarian "which of these are public?"

**What happens to my dumps if I'm offline?**
They queue on your device and sync when the connection is back. The dump box always accepts input — it never eats your words.

**How do I get a buddy for something new?**
Ask in the main chat. Creation picks sensible defaults (parent, persona, icon) without making you choose — you refine them later if you want.

# Buddy System Software Manual

**Owner:** the Documentarian (📚) — maintains this manual and the master index it describes.
**Status:** Draft v1 — 2026-10-16. Built from the registry, the repos, and the artifact catalog — not from memory. Anything unverified is flagged inline rather than invented.

This is the operator's manual for the whole buddy system: the data structures, the visibility rules, the build pipeline, the homepage system, the buddy lifecycle, and the standing rules that shape how it all behaves. When something about the system needs re-explaining, the answer should be findable here first.

---

## 1. The Registry (buddies.json)

### What it is

`buddies.json` is the single source of truth for buddy **metadata**. One JSON file at `~/workspace/projects/buddy-tree/buddies.json` (also mirrored inside the built dashboard page). As of 2026-10-16 it holds 111 entries. Everything the dashboard renders — the tree, the cards, the info panel, the links — comes from this file.

### Anatomy of an entry

Every entry carries these fields (verified against the live registry):

| Field | What it holds |
|---|---|
| `id` | Kebab-case unique key, e.g. `documentarian-buddy`. Never changes; URLs, repos, and threads key off it. |
| `name` | Display name, e.g. `Documentarian`. Renamable (see §5). |
| `kind` | Category: `standalone`, `family`, `personal`, `people`, `property`, `assistant`, `finance`, `work`, `meta`, `life`, `root`, `buddy`. (Some older entries lack it.) |
| `parent` | The id of the parent buddy, or `null` for top-level. Defines the tree. |
| `status` / `statusClass` | Lifecycle state, e.g. `New`, `Mission review`, `active`, `Live · pre-alpha`. Free-text in practice — see the open question on retirement below. |
| `tagline` | One-line description shown on cards and homepages. |
| `mission` | The buddy's mission statement text (short form). |
| `brief` | URL of the product brief (Google Doc). |
| `repo` | GitHub repo short name, e.g. `documentarian-buddy` → `davedellaquila/documentarian-buddy`. 26 buddies (mostly person/family entries) have none. Four entries store a full URL instead of a name — a hygiene issue, not a breakage. |
| `docs` | Array of `{label, url}` — mission statements, briefs, and other linked documents. |
| `homepage` | Path of the buddy's dashboard homepage, e.g. `documentarian-buddy/`, served under `https://davedellaquila.github.io/buddy-system/`. Every buddy currently has one. |
| `icon` | Emoji shown across the system. |
| `photos` | Array (usually empty) for photo attachments. |
| `persona` | Short persona label, e.g. `Meticulous archivist`. |
| `links` | Many-to-many buddy links (`links[]`) — the "everything links to everything" graph, rendered as Linked Buddies with automatic reverse links. |
| `attention` | Array of `{date, id, text, url}` — open items needing Dave's input, rendered as attention tiles. |

### What the registry tracks — and what it does NOT

It tracks: identity, hierarchy, display metadata, docs, repos, homepages, links, attention items, lifecycle status.

It does **not** track:

- **Visibility.** There is no `public`/`private` field on a registry entry. The registry cannot tell you who can see a page, and it never decides who can see what. This is the central visibility gap — see §2.
- **Page content.** Homepages live as HTML files beside the registry, not inside it.
- **Artifact share states.** Whether a web artifact is publicly shared lives in the artifact system, not the registry.
- **Secrets.** Tokens stay on-device (see §4); the registry never holds credentials.

---

## 2. Visibility model

Three layers. They are independent — do not assume one implies another.

### Layer A — Web artifacts: real private/public

Web artifacts (the `muse.ai/s/…` pages) have **actual access control** in the artifact system. Each artifact is either shared (public link works for anyone with the URL) or unshared (owner-only). This is the only layer with technical enforcement.

The Documentarian audits every artifact's share state against the **known-public list** (§2c). As of 2026-10-16: 31 web artifacts, 8 public, 23 private.

### Layer B — Buddy homepages: policy, not access control

Buddy homepages live at `https://davedellaquila.github.io/buddy-system/<buddy-id>/` — a **public GitHub Pages site with no authentication**. Anyone with the URL can open any buddy homepage.

Dave's standing rule — *"buddy-published pages stay private unless I explicitly make one public, one page at a time"* — is therefore **policy, not technology**: "private" means *we don't share the URL*, not *the URL is protected*. Everyone operating the system must understand this distinction. Never treat an unshared homepage URL as a secret, and never put anything on a homepage that would be harmful if the URL leaked.

### Layer C — The known-public list

The list of web artifacts Dave has explicitly approved as public. As of 2026-10-16, eight entries:

1. Wildlife Trail Hill Happenings (`area-events`)
2. Buddy OS Teaser (`buddy-os-teaser`)
3. Cabin Retreat Teaser (`cabin-retreat-teaser`)
4. Legal Department (`legal-department`)
5. Work Buddy (`work-buddy`)
6. Zen (`zen`)
7. Buddy Directory (`buddy-directory`) — approved 2026-10-16
8. Events Master List (`events-master-list`) — approved 2026-10-16

**Where it lives:** in Astro's memory and in the Documentarian's master index (`index-v1.json`, field `known_public`).
**How entries get added:** Dave's explicit call only — one page at a time. The Documentarian flags anything publicly shared that isn't on the list; it never adds entries itself and never changes share states.
**How entries get removed:** same — Dave's call; the Documentarian records the removal.

---

## 3. The dashboard

### The build pipeline

The dashboard is a static site generated by `build.py` in `~/workspace/projects/buddy-tree/`:

1. **Reads:** `buddies.json` (the registry) + `buddy-tree.html` (styles and the three view fragments).
2. **Writes:** `index.html` — the full dashboard with the registry embedded — and `version.txt` (a build-number stamp, format `YYYYMMDD.HHMM`).
3. **Deploy:** committed and pushed to `davedellaquila/buddy-system`; GitHub Pages serves it at `https://davedellaquila.github.io/buddy-system/`.

### The deploy ritual (standing rules)

- **Wait at least 2 minutes after pushing** before telling Dave anything is live — GitHub Pages needs the time.
- **Verify on the served copy** (curl the live URL) before announcing. The record is the only receipt.
- **Tell Dave to hard-refresh** — his browser may be holding the old version.
- The dashboard itself checks `version.txt` on load and shows an **update banner** when a newer build exists (suppressed for 10 minutes after "Update now" is clicked, to give Pages time).

### The project-buddy artifact mirror

The `project-buddy` web artifact mirrors the GitHub Pages dashboard. After every dashboard change, it is refreshed via `artifact.edit` with a verbatim request to fetch the live dashboard URL and mirror it exactly — **but only after confirming the Pages deployment is live** (check with curl first, so the builder fetches the new version).

> ⚠️ Known limitation (2026-10-13): the mirror builder can refuse pages whose client-side flows trip builder policy (it refused once over the news-buddy GitHub-token flow). The live site is unaffected when this happens; only the mirror goes stale. Surface the failure to Dave.

### CHANGELOG discipline

Every shipped feature gets a `CHANGELOG.md` entry in the buddy-tree repo, same commit or right after. `FEATURES.md` describes the current state; `CHANGELOG.md` describes how it got there. Never let them drift.

---

## 4. Homepage system

Every buddy has a homepage at `https://davedellaquila.github.io/buddy-system/<buddy-id>/` (the `homepage` field in the registry).

### Template vs custom pages

- **Template pages** are generated by `gen_basic_homepages.py` and carry a `BUDDY-BASIC-TEMPLATE` marker. Regenerating refreshes them — safe to rebuild any time.
- **Custom pages** are thread-owned designs carrying a `<!-- CUSTOM HOMEPAGE -->` marker. **The generator never overwrites them**, even on regen. (Rule set 2026-10-15 after a custom page was once clobbered.)

### Standing page requirements

- **Slim sticky header** (Dave, 2026-10-15): icon left of the page name, content scrolls underneath — Dave always knows which page he's on.
- **Working dark mode** (standing product requirement): every buddy page ships dark mode — `prefers-color-scheme` or an explicit theme toggle. Light-only pages are not done.
- **Brain-dump box** (standard): every homepage has one (see below).

### Name-sync

Custom homepages stay in sync with registry renames without a rebuild:

- Elements carrying `data-buddy-name` are populated from a live fetch of `../buddies.json` at page load.
- Pages also listen for the `buddyTree.rename` storage event, so an open page updates its displayed name **without a refresh** when a rename lands.

### The dump box

The 🧠 brain-dump box on each homepage (and the dashboard info panel) appends a timestamped entry to the buddy's repo at `docs/ingest.md`, which the buddy later triages.

- **Token-gated write-through:** the write goes through the GitHub contents API using a personal access token. With a token connected, dumps land in the repo immediately.
- **Local queue fallback** (widened 2026-10-16): the box **always accepts input**. With no token and/or no repo linked, dumps save to the device's `localStorage` and the status says so plainly. The backlog flushes into `docs/ingest.md` on the next successful GitHub dump.
- **Cmd/Ctrl+Enter** submits from the textarea.

### The token model

- The GitHub token is pasted once by Dave (dashboard gear menu → GitHub token; homepage "Connect GitHub" flow) and stored **on-device only**, in `localStorage` (`buddyTree.v3` → `ghToken`). It is never sent to Astro, never stored in the registry, repos, or memory.
- **Queued writes flush at next sync:** renames (`S.pendingRenames`), dumps (`pendingDumps` / per-buddy local queues) — anything the token-gated path couldn't write immediately retries once a token is present.
- Without a token, everything degrades to on-device state with plain-English status. Nothing is silently dropped.

---

## 5. Buddy lifecycle

### Create

A new buddy comes into being with four (sometimes five) artifacts:

1. **Registry entry** in `buddies.json` — id, name, kind, parent, tagline, mission, icon, repo, docs, brief, homepage path.
2. **Homepage** — template-generated, or a custom design owned by the buddy's thread.
3. **Repo** — `davedellaquila/<buddy-id>`, private, with the standard file set (`SOUL.md`, `README.md`, `KNOWLEDGE.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `ACCOMPLISHMENTS.md`, `docs/mission-statement.md`, `docs/ingest.md`).
4. **Thread** — the buddy's side chat, where it works.
5. **Dashboard rebuild** — so the new entry appears (the buddy list on the Buddy Directory page self-updates via its live registry fetch).

Buddy-creation defaults (Dave, 2026-10-15): never block creation on parent/host, persona, or icon choices — pick sensible defaults and move; Dave changes them later if he wants.

### Rename

1. Dave renames in the dashboard info panel (or a homepage name control).
2. `writeGlobalName()` edits the registry entry's `name` **via the token-gated GitHub path** — it reads `buddies.json` from GitHub, applies all queued renames, and writes it back.
3. **Without a token**, the rename queues in `S.pendingRenames` (on-device only) and the in-memory entry updates immediately so it *feels* instant on that device.
4. `flushPendingRenames()` pushes the queue through on the next sync once a token is connected.
5. Open homepages pick it up live via the `buddyTree.rename` storage event; others read the new name from the registry on next load.

### Retire

> ⚠️ **Open question — unverified.** The registry has no formal `retired` status; observed statuses are free-text (`New`, `Mission review`, `active`, `Live · pre-alpha`, …). There is no documented retire flow (what happens to the homepage, repo, thread, and links when a buddy is retired). This section will be written once Dave defines it. The Documentarian will not invent one.

---

## 6. Standing rules that shape behavior

These standing rules from `AGENTS.md` directly affect how the system works (conversational habits omitted):

- **Artifact sync** — the `project-buddy` web artifact mirrors the dashboard after every change; verify Pages is live before mirroring (§3).
- **CHANGELOG discipline** — every shipped feature gets a `CHANGELOG.md` entry, same commit or right after (§3).
- **Google Docs deliverables** — product briefs, PRDs, and system docs live **only** as Google Docs in the Buddy Docs Drive folder. Never `.docx` files, not even as staging. Dave doesn't use Word.
- **"Have the information come to me"** (Dave, 2026-10-15) — when a question has logical answer choices, put them inline where Dave already is: tappable buttons, options in the message. Deciding never requires a detour. This shapes attention tiles, review prompts, and blocker relays across the whole system.
- **Document links** (Dave, 2026-10-07 — foundational): anything Dave can click on gets its link, every time it's mentioned. Applies to status messages too — re-link every time.
- **Review prompt on every deliverable** (Dave, 2026-10-08): when something is finished, ask Dave to review it, with quick replies.
- **Dashboard link on every code update** (Dave, 2026-10-12): report a dashboard change → include the dashboard link so he can load his local copy.
- **Message footers** (Dave, 2026-10-15): every message to Dave ends with a footer linking the current buddy's dashboard.
- **Audio on-demand** (Dave, 2026-10-16): no auto-TTS; "🔊 Listen to this" is a standard quick-reply option.

---

## 7. Pro tips: how to think about the system

Reference material tells you what the system *is*. This section is about how to *use* it well — the mental model and the power-user workflows, in Dave's words where they exist.

### The mental model

> "A buddy is the smallest unit of your staff: one slice of your life with a thread you can talk to, a repo that remembers everything, a homepage showing its current state, and an inbox it triages. They're all wired together, so anything you dump anywhere finds the right desk. Less 'app,' more 'person who never forgets and never sleeps.'"

Three consequences of that model:

1. **Don't organize — delegate.** You don't file things into the system. You hand things to a buddy and *it* files, remembers, and surfaces them. If you're building folders in your head, you're doing the buddy's job.
2. **Talk to the slice, not the system.** Each buddy owns one slice of your life. When something comes up, ask which slice it belongs to and take it to that buddy's thread. The wiring between buddies handles the rest.
3. **Every object has its own homepage, configurable to that object.** People, cars, properties, projects — anything that matters gets a buddy, and every buddy gets a homepage showing its current state. If you want to know where something stands, go to its homepage.

### Talk, don't type

Dictating to your buddy is part of onboarding — Dave's words: Wispr Flow is *"really the best dictation software I've used to date — certainly better than the built-in dictation software in either iOS or the Mac, or Muse for that matter."*

- **iPhone:** Wispr Flow.
- **iPad:** Superwhisper (Wispr Flow's mobile build is iPhone-only; Superwhisper covers iPhone and iPad).
- **Caveat** (observed 2026-10-15): phone dictation can garble. If a dictated message arrives looking off, ask before acting on it — don't guess through the garble.

### Dump anywhere, raw

The brain-dump box takes **raw, unfiltered thought** — half-formed is fine, polished is unnecessary. The buddy triages; you don't pre-organize. And because the buddies are wired together, anything you dump anywhere finds the right desk. The highest-value habit in the whole system: when something crosses your mind, dump it into the nearest box instead of carrying it.

Cmd/Ctrl+Enter submits from the textarea.

### Main chat as dispatch

Dave's operating pattern: **the main chat is the dispatch thread.** Side threads do the work, but anything needing Dave comes to the main chat — blockers arrive as the question plus its quick replies, inline, where he already is. He doesn't go hunting through side threads, and neither should you: if you're in a side thread waiting on Dave, the relay to the main chat is the move.

### Let the information come to you

Dave's rule: *"have the information come to me."* The system is built around it — questions arrive with their answer choices inline, attention tiles carry tappable buttons, review prompts come with quick replies. The corollary is a bug detector: **if you find yourself going somewhere to answer something, that's a bug worth reporting.**

### Power workflows

- **Connect the GitHub token once** (dashboard gear menu → GitHub token). It's stored on-device only, and from then on renames, dumps, and every queued write sync through to the repos and the registry.
- **After a dashboard push:** wait 2 minutes, then hard-refresh. Or skip the wait entirely — load it locally: `python3 -m http.server 8000` in the buddy-system clone, then `localhost:8000` (needs http, not `file://` — the page fetches `version.txt`).
- **Answer inline.** Bounded questions always come with tappable quick replies — one tap, no typing. "Let's workshop this" and "Let's whiteboard this" are always among the options when you want to riff instead of answer.
- **Check the Documentarian's index before building.** New buddy, new page, new doc — ask the index first. Nothing built twice, nothing lost.

# STORY.md — the shape

Every person buddy gets a `STORY.md` in its repo, alongside the other desk files
(`KNOWLEDGE.md`, `CONFIG.md`, `STUDY.md`). It is the place where that person's
background and life stories accumulate. Whenever we learn something about
someone's past, it goes in their story.

Follows the desk-file conventions: `# <Name> — Story` title, one-line purpose
statement, `##` sections, bullets and short told anecdotes. Dave (or the buddy)
edits it directly; the desk renders it read-only like the other files.

## Template

```markdown
# <Name> — Story

<Name>'s background and life stories, as told and learned over time.
Whenever we learn something about <Name>'s past, it goes here.

## Background

### Origins
Where they come from — birth, childhood, the people and places that shaped them.

### Career
What they've done with their working life.

### Family
The people closest to them.

## Stories

### <Story title> — <era or date>
Told anecdotes, in the teller's voice where we have it. 2–5 sentences:
the moment, not just the fact. One `###` per story; newest last.

## Still to tell
Threads they've promised but haven't told yet — the buddy may nudge them.
```

## Conventions

- **Background** holds settled facts (origins / career / family). **Stories** holds
  told anecdotes with a title and era. **Still to tell** holds promised-but-untold
  threads so the buddy can nudge ("you said you'd tell me about Skeleton").
- Date-stamp new entries `(YYYY-MM-DD)` when the telling date is known.
- Mark uncertainty explicitly: "Dave's words", "unconfirmed", "dictation uncertain".
  Never invent biography.
- One story per `###`, newest appended last — the file reads as an accumulating
  life, not a wiki article.
- People who are gone (Emil, Martha) still get one: background + whatever stories
  survive. The story is the memorial.

## Standing rule (Dave, 2026-10-17)

"When we learn about somebody and something happened in their past, add it to
their story." Applies to every person buddy, going forward.

## Chapter variant (Dave, 2026-10-17)

Dave's own STORY.md uses a chapter spine instead of the Background/Stories split:
**growing up → high school → college → Silicon Valley → the cabin.** Facts and
told stories file under whichever chapter they belong to; `####` story subheads
sit inside their chapter; `## Still to tell` stays at the end. The structure is
the deliverable — chapter contents accumulate over time, never invented. Other
person buddies keep the default shape unless Dave gives them chapters too.

# Buddy System User Guide

*Welcome! This is the short, friendly version — the welcome packet, not the operator's manual. (There* is *a full Software Manual for the technically curious; your buddy can point you to it.)*

---

## Welcome

You're trying out something new. Instead of one giant app that tries to do everything, you get a small staff of helpers called **buddies** — each one looking after a single slice of your life.

One buddy might keep track of your home projects. Another might help with planning. Each buddy is a specialist, and they all work together behind the scenes.

## The big idea

Here's the one idea that makes everything else click:

> A buddy is the smallest unit of your staff: one slice of your life with a thread you can talk to, a record of everything it knows, a homepage showing its current state, and an inbox it triages. Less "app," more "person who never forgets and never sleeps."

In plain words: imagine an assistant who is completely devoted to one area of your life, never sleeps, never forgets anything you tell it, and always shows you exactly where things stand. That's a buddy.

Three things follow from that:

1. **You don't organize — you delegate.** You don't file things into the system. You just hand things to your buddy and *it* files, remembers, and brings things back at the right time. If you catch yourself building folders in your head, stop — that's your buddy's job.
2. **Talk to the right buddy.** Each buddy owns one slice. When something comes up, take it to the buddy whose slice it belongs to. They handle the rest together.
3. **Check the homepage to see where things stand.** Every buddy has its own homepage showing the current state of its slice — open questions, what's done, what's next. When you wonder "where are we on this?", the homepage is the answer.

## Getting set up

It takes about two minutes:

1. **Open your buddy's chat.** You'll get a link — that's your direct line. Bookmark it.
2. **Say hi.** Tell your buddy what you want help with, in your own words. There's no wrong way to start.
3. **If it asks you to connect anything, follow along.** It only takes a minute, and nothing is ever shared with anyone without your explicit say-so.

That's it. You're set up.

## How to talk to your buddy

- **Type or dictate — both are fine.** On iPhone, the dictation app Wispr Flow is excellent for longer thoughts; on iPad, Superwhisper works well. But typing works just as well. Use whatever's at hand.
- **Be raw.** Half-formed thoughts are perfect. "The thing with the plumber, ugh, need to deal with that" is a completely valid message. Your buddy sorts it out — you don't need to be polished or organized.
- **One tap answers.** When your buddy asks you something with tappable options, just tap one. No typing needed.

## The dump box: your new superpower

Every buddy homepage has a **brain-dump box**. It's a simple text box with one job: catch whatever crosses your mind.

The highest-value habit in the whole system is this: **when something pops into your head, dump it into the nearest box instead of carrying it around.** A name, a worry, a half-remembered errand, a link — dump it raw. Your buddy files it, remembers it, and brings it back when it matters.

Nothing you put in the box is ever lost. If your buddy can't save it right away, it waits safely on your device and syncs later.

## Your homepage

Your buddy's homepage is its front door — the current state of your slice, in one glance:

- What's open and needs your input
- What's been done
- Key links and documents

One important note: **your homepage is a sneak peek, not a finished product.** The system is still being built, and your pages are private to you — they're not for sharing or forwarding. If you ever want something made public, that's a separate conversation, one page at a time.

## Your privacy, in plain words

- **Your buddies and pages are yours.** Nothing is shared with anyone without your explicit say-so.
- **Drafts never send on their own.** If your buddy drafts a message, email, or post for you, nothing goes out until you tap approve. That's a hard rule.
- **Your words stay yours.** What you tell your buddy is used to help *you*, not anyone else.

## Quick tips

- **Something look stale?** Refresh the page. The system updates constantly, and your browser sometimes shows yesterday's version.
- **Wondering where something is?** Just ask your buddy "where is X?" — it keeps the map.
- **Got an idea for the system itself?** Tell your buddy. Feedback from testers is how this gets better.

## Getting help

Just ask your buddy. That's what it's for. And if it can't help with something, it'll say so honestly — it never bluffs.

Welcome aboard. 🤝

# Documentarian

## Executive summary

The Documentarian is the buddy system's memory of itself: a meticulous archivist that owns the master index of everything the system has produced — every buddy, web page, Google Doc, repo, artifact, and link — with visibility status (private vs public) and change history. When Dave asks "do we have X?", "where is Y?", or "which of these are public?", the Documentarian answers from the index, never from guesswork. It keeps the Buddy Directory page current and never loses anything.

## The idea

Dave's system has grown past what any one mind can hold: 109 buddies, 30 web artifacts, dozens of Google Docs and repos, and a web of links between them — and no one owns the map. Links live in buddies.json, docs in Drive, artifacts scattered across the catalog, history in Astro's memory. Dave keeps asking "do we have X?" and "which pages are public?" The Documentarian is the single owner of the cross-system index and the historian of what changed when.

## How it works

The Documentarian maintains the master index from authoritative sources, not memory: the buddy registry (buddies.json) for buddies, the artifact catalog for web pages (each badged private or public from its share state), the Buddy Docs Drive folder for documents, GitHub (davedellaquila/<buddy-id>, all private) for repos, and buddies.json links[] for the relationship graph. It keeps the Buddy Directory page current as buddies and pages are added — Astro built v1, the Documentarian owns it from here. It records change history: what was built, changed, published, shared, or renamed, and when. It upholds Dave's standing rule: buddy-published pages are private unless Dave explicitly makes one public, one page at a time — and flags anything that looks externally visible without his approval.

## Deconfliction with Document Buddy

Document Buddy (📄) owns the Google Docs layer: organizing Drive folders and tracking per-doc metadata. The Documentarian does not touch folder organization. It indexes documents as entries in the master index (title, link, which buddy they belong to) and records their history. Document Buddy is a source; the Documentarian is the map.

## Revenue potential

Internal staff, not a product: no direct revenue. Its value is time saved (no more hunting for links), nothing lost (nothing built twice), and safety (no private page ever mistaken for public).

## Room for growth

Drift detection (index vs reality, on a schedule); link health checks; auto-refreshing the Buddy Directory; a periodic "what changed" digest for Dave; indexing side-thread decisions.

## Out of scope for v1

No folder reorganization (Document Buddy's job). No editing the dashboard build itself. No auto-publishing or auto-sharing anything — visibility changes are Dave's call only. No inventing entries: everything indexed must exist and be verifiable.

## Open questions

Should the index live as versioned JSON in the buddy's repo? Should it send Dave a weekly "what changed" digest, or only answer on demand? When a new buddy is spun up, should the drill hand the Documentarian the entry automatically?

# Ingest log — Documentarian

Raw brain-dumps about this buddy. Each entry is timestamped; work the keepers into the dossier.

🧠 Brain dump

Dump what's on your mind about Documentarian — raw and unfiltered. Each dump lands in the buddy's repo (docs/ingest.md) as a timestamped entry — or saves on this device until a repo is linked and a token is connected.
